# Production readiness — single task list (2026-10-11)

**This is the one open-work list.** It supersedes `docs/PROD_READY_CHECKLIST.md` (last full update 2026-10-04) and
`docs/plans/PLAN.md` §3–§5 as the *status* source. The acceptance criteria it is built on are in
`docs/audits/RELEASE_EVIDENCE_2026-10-11_CLAUDE_CODE.md` ("Acceptance criteria" and "Railway readiness gate"); the invariants
are in `CLAUDE.md`. Do not start another plan file: edit this one. Companion (research session, 2026-10-12): `docs/plans/BEST_ANSWER_TASK_LIST_2026-10-12.md` was removed per owner's word 2026-10-13 (§20); its answer-quality rows live folded in §9/§12 below, and this list stays the single list.

## Ruthless order of work (2026-10-15 night; this block wins over any older ordering below)

Verdict stays **NO-GO** until a clean-Docker run on final `main` is graded. Order: faculty-ready, then prod-ready, then Railway. A row is closed only with its evidence file named here. "Tree" = uncommitted in the working tree; nothing below is pushed.

**Correction 2026-10-19:** "nothing below is pushed" no longer holds — PR #68 (`feat/clip-quality-grading` → `main`, which carried the whole A-block) **merged 2026-10-17**, and PRs #69–#73 merged after it; `origin/main` head is `f7ea8d8c`. Rows A10, A34, A37, A39 and the duplicate A25/A26 rows are therefore **closed**; A21 (CodeQL) is the only one still awaiting a re-check after a push. The verdict stays **NO-GO**: Railway is untouched and paused, nothing is deployed, and the human gates (A11/A12, section B) are unchanged.

(b) **Residual seed gap now fixed in tree (2026-10-19):** scoping only the hops still let a name collision resolve the *seed* to another tenant's node, and the `RETURN` payload hands back that node's own name/description. The seed match is now `WHERE (seed.entity_id = seed_id OR seed.name = seed_id) AND coalesce(seed.tenant_id, $default_tenant_id) = $tenant_id`, and the **index-global** vector seed is filtered after `YIELD` (`WITH seed, similarity WHERE coalesce(seed.tenant_id, $default_tenant_id) = $tenant_id`) — fail closed, smaller seed set rather than a foreign seed. Untagged nodes coalesce to the default tenant, so single-tenant behaviour is unchanged. Nothing broken, nothing changed. (d) **Purge dispatch analysed, deliberately NOT built** (owner approval required; full analysis in the subagent report mirrored to `.work/SESSION_STATE_2026-10-19.md`). Real gap found: `app/api/compliance.py` never sets `TenantContext`, but `memory_service_v2.purge_all_user_data` scopes its Qdrant/Neo4j deletes by `TenantContext.get() or "default"` — so an admin purge runs under the *caller's* tenant and can silently delete nothing in Qdrant/Neo4j while returning a success receipt; and a partial purge still reports `deletion_completed` because `result["errors"]` is not escalated. Invisible today (single tenant). Also missing from the purge scope: Second Brain vault, `push_devices`, feedback/telemetry, S3 artefacts. (b) mobile-Safari autoplay **NO FALLBACK and NOT VERIFIED** — no WebKit binary is installed locally, so `p3` is Chromium with an iPhone-12 UA/viewport; it exercises layout + the blocked path but **not** WebKit's autoplay policy. **Shared-tree rules (three sessions have written to one checkout; L-ONE-WRITER-1):** (1) never `git switch`/`checkout`/`stash`/`reset`; the tree is one checkout. (2) Commit by explicit path only (`git commit -m ... -- <paths>`), never `git add -A` or `commit -a`; the index is shared. (3) Do not push, and do not commit unless the owner asked. (4) The repo is public: no transcript or clip text, audio, `.env` or database dumps in any commit. (5) Do not restart, recreate or rebuild `mukthiguru-backend`, flush Redis or touch `.env` without telling the first-person session and the owner. (6) `lessons.md` is prepend-only. Backend code changes need only `docker restart` (source is bind-mounted); dependency or `.env` changes need a recreate, and `.env` edits need a backup and a `diff` restore check. (7) One list: edit rows here, do not start another plan file (L-ONE-LIST-1). (8) Edit this file one session at a time: ask the previous writer to commit first.

**Lanes:** *first-person lane* = `first_person_bridge.py`, `first_person_pipeline.py`, `quote_weaver.py`, `build_first_person_index.py`, `grade_clip_quality.py`, `evals/gold_set_v1/`, first-person flags in `config.py` and `.env`, backend container restarts (section 8 is its handoff). *CI and task-list lane* = workflows, Trivy, `requirements.lock`, `Dockerfile`, `coalescer.py`, `lessons.md` (prepend), this file, the frontend half of T-B14. *Research lane* = `reports/` (git-ignored), no code. ## 0. Verdict

| Scope | State | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Faculty preview (password-gated) | **NO-GO** — no time estimate given: it depends on owner actions (T-A6 role split, T-B3, T-B5, T-B6, sign-off) whose dates are not set; the host is decided (Railway, 2026-10-12) but nothing is deployed (T-B9) | Core path works locally and is honest. Blocked by: not deployed anywhere, **the answer rate (see the 2026-10-19 note below: gate-driven, not a corpus ceiling)**, voice-verification coverage (1,116 of 1,801 v8 clips are not voice-verified; 685 were, per the older count), no faculty reviewer named. |
| Testers (friendly external) | **NO-GO** | Tester-ready = Faculty GO **plus** per-person access (today: one shared code, no revocation), consent recorded, a feedback channel (T-B12), and a per-user cost cap that does not fail open when Redis is down (it does today). WhatsApp testers also need T-C8/T-C9. |
| Public users | **NO-GO, far** | Needs the human work that has never been started: 299 gold labels, clinician review, native-speaker review, helpline phone checks, rights sign-off. |

What works today, locally (quoted from the evidence report, not re-run): `/api/chat` answers with a teacher's own recorded clip
or abstains; 0 of 13 out-of-corpus questions served a clip; crisis, abuse and medication probes route to helplines; backend
9,918 tests and frontend 812 tests pass; 23 of 24 served clips match YouTube's own captions.

What a user would actually experience today: about 3 in 4 on-topic questions get "I don't have a verified verbatim teaching
that directly answers this", answers take p50 6.9 s / p95 8.7 s, every answer is labelled "Related, not a direct answer", and
most clips show "unverified clip" (685 of 1,801 clips in `first_person_v8` are voice-verified, per `CLAUDE.md` invariant 23).

**Update 2026-10-19 (first-person session, local only; Railway untouched and still paused).** Two corrections to the picture above, both measured, neither a production claim:

1. **The low answer rate is a CLIP-CONTENT-GATE effect — not a corpus ceiling, not attribution, not the distress rule. This supersedes the earlier "149/150 dry-showable" reading.** A dry evaluator (`backend/scripts/ops/eval_v8_vs_v9.py`) reports **149/150 on-topic questions with at least one dry-showable clip** — but it deliberately **skips the LLM gates**, so that number is a statement about retrieval + integrity + content quality, not about what a seeker receives. The **live** measurement (150-question benign on-topic pool, `POST /api/chat`, benchmark identity, `first_person_v9`, cache cold, `trace_id` joined per question) is: **26 served (17%) / 103 abstained / 17 distress lane / 2 crisis-preempted / 2 tier-2**. Reasons: `relevance_below_min` **94**, `answerability_withheld` **8**, `llm_fallback_disabled` **102**. Two independent sweeps agreed to ±2 questions (only `q063`, `q124` flipped; both served→abstained), so treat ±2 as LLM-gate boundary flakiness, not signal.
   **The decisive number — joined, per question, on the pipeline's own `n_quarantined`:**

   | candidates the content-quality gate removed | questions | served | serve rate |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | **0** | 32 | 24 | **75.0%** |
   | 4–7 | 24 | 0 | 0.0% |
   | 8–11 | 51 | 2 | 3.9% |
   | 12+ | 21 | 0 | 0.0% |

   Pooled: `n_quarantined == 0` → **24/32 served (75%)**; `n_quarantined > 0` → **2/96 served (2.1%)** — a 36× separation on one directly-observed variable that sits *upstream* of both LLM gates (they only ever see the survivors). Upstream of it, **842 content-quality quarantine events** across those 128 questions (median 8/question, max 16): **`>120s cap` 292 (35%)**, **`not standalone (orphan_opener)` 287 (34%)**, `live-event instruction pattern` 108, `ends_mid_sentence` 78, `health_claim` 52, `event_marker` 13, `only N words (min 15)` 11, `parable character reference` 1. **The top two are 69% of everything the gate rejects, and both are passage-assembly defects at ingest.**
   Sizing: only **134 of 1,981** clips (7%) exceed the 120 s serve cap, but they are heavily retrieved and generate 292 of the 842 events; clip durations are median 38 s, p75 72 s, max 180 s. The honest ceiling for any ingest fix is the never-quarantined bucket's **75%**. See `lessons.md` → `L-FP-LIVE-FUNNEL-1`, `L-FP-CONTENT-GATE-1`.
   **Not claimed:** that recovering a quarantined candidate makes *that* question serve — a recovered candidate still has to clear relevance and answerability. What is proven is where the loss happens first, and that it is 36×.
2. **Attribution is NOT what holds the answer rate down (measured 2026-10-19, corrects the row-A17 framing).** All **1,981** `first_person_v9` points carry `first_person_eligible=True` and `is_verbatim=True`, including the **1,202 with `speaker_verified` absent**. Neither the serve-time integrity gate (`_passes_integrity_gate`) nor `_eligible_citations` reads `speaker_verified`; it is a **provenance/display claim only** (absent → not stamped → UI shows `shared in <channel>` instead of naming the teacher). Live counts: v8 = 1,801 total / 685 verified / 1,116 absent; v9 = 1,981 / 779 / 1,202. So the voice-recheck work improves attribution honesty and can keep host audio out of served spans, but it moves the serve rate by **zero questions**. Related: trimming the unverified clips to a clean per-window run **is** mechanically feasible — a span synthesised from the clean run was re-verified against the real `verify_clip_speaker_strict` and passes **1,116/1,116**; **920 (82%) have a clean run ≥8 s**, 827 ≥10 s, 638 ≥15 s, median 17 s (524 fail on exactly one stray window). That is an attribution fix, not a coverage fix. See `lessons.md` → `L-FP-SPEAKER-STRICT-GATE-1`, `L-FP-TRIM-SPAN-1`.
3. **The distress decline is real but small next to the above.** `first_person_bridge._bridge()` declines at `_DISTRESS_DECLINE_LEVEL = 2` (MODERATE) on a **single emotion keyword** (`broken`, `angry`, `fear`, `anxiety`, `worried`, `unhappy`, `overwhelmed`, `panic`, `gussa`). **17 of the 150** benign on-topic questions route to the distress lane (14 of them had clips waiting). This is **collection-independent, pre-dates v9**, and is the owner's call. **Not changed without clinician review** (A7/A12/A16). Offline router margin data, for the record: on-topic DISTRESS hijacks 32 at threshold 0.66, 10 at 0.72, but at **0.74+** `"I am suicidal"` stops matching the router (still caught by the guardrail `self_harm` rail and `assess_distress`), so the boundary must not be tuned without a clinician pass. **Industry-practice check (2026-10-19; full brief at `lessons.md` → `L-FP-DISTRESS-TIER-1`):** documented shipping products tier on *self-directed risk*, not on emotion, and serve content **plus** a dismissible support affordance — replacing the answer with boilerplate is the one behaviour the published evidence attributes direct harm to. NICE NG225 §1.6.3 says do not stratify into low/medium/high; Franklin et al. (2016, 365 studies) put single-marker prediction at "only slightly better than chance". This evidence supports the owner's chosen additive option (compassion **plus** the clip at MODERATE).

Also on 2026-10-19 (local): `first_person_v9` (1,981 pts = v8's 1,801 + 180 mass-ingest clips) was built and promoted to **local serving** (`FIRST_PERSON_COLLECTION=first_person_v9`), with `first_person_v8` left intact at 1,801 for rollback. Promotion evidence: all 38 evaluable frozen questions keep an identical top-1 clip on a saturated bank; a fresh 28-question delta bank scores v9 top-1 0.694 vs v8 0.671; all channels are rights-cleared. Real remaining limit: **clip quality** (~1 in 7 stored clips is a clean, self-contained teaching, per the blind judge above) — that, not coverage, is the binding constraint on the data side.

**Update 2026-10-11 (reported by the v9/gold-set session, not re-checked here):** the v9 "complete thoughts" rebuild did not
help. A blind independent judge on 142 clips (version hidden) scored complete-at-both-ends 24% → 27% and good clips 13% → 15%,
so only about 1 in 7 stored clips is a clean, self-contained teaching. **Whole-corpus grading (2026-10-11, finished) confirms the order of magnitude:** of 1,801 v8 clips, Haiku grade A = 336 (18.7%), B = 406, C = 1,035, 24 ungradable; a strict Opus second pass kept only 119 of the 336 A clips (6.6% of the corpus). The 2026-10-11 "complete thoughts" v9 build is withdrawn (T-B2) — **naming note:** that withdrawn rebuild is *not* the 2026-10-19 `first_person_v9` mass-ingest collection described in the update above, which is what the local stack now serves; the real fix is re-segmentation
and clip grading (T-B13). Calibration also needs about 1,450 questions asked (labelled top-1 queries for a 1% bound, not 299; ~1,450 questions asked is a floor, not labels count; see RC-E-3/RC-E-4). Both
push the faculty and public dates out: the binding constraint is clip quality in the data.

## 1. Why three to four months has not produced a production system

These are causes read off the repo's own record, in order of how much time each one cost.

1. **The product definition changed three times.** A 20-node LLM RAG pipeline (CRAG, CoVe, HyDE, GraphRAG, LightRAG, OKF,
   memory) was built and tuned from July to September. On 2026-09-24 the priority became verbatim first-person clips. On
   2026-10-11 chat became first-person-only. The knowledge graph, LightRAG and most of the generation and verification nodes
   are now off the serving path (evidence report, Opus audit 2). Months of latency and faithfulness tuning apply to a path
   users no longer reach.
2. **The critical path is human work, and it was never commissioned.** Calibration needs 299 human-labelled pairs; 14 exist.
   Clinician review, native-speaker review and helpline phone checks have been "pending" since September. No agent can close
   these, and without them the system cannot give a "direct answer" or be called safe. Agent sessions kept producing code
   around a gate that only a person can open.
3. **The ceiling is in the data, not the code.** Only 15 of 29 test questions have even one showable clip in the top 10. Clips
   start mid-thought, end mid-sentence, include host speech, and 46% of v7 was stale orphans. Selection logic was tuned for
   weeks against a ceiling set by segmentation.
4. **Nothing has been deployed since August.** Railway's backend has been in a failed state since 2026-08-23
   (**[checked today]** `railway status` → Failed; the public URL returns 404; `api.askmukthiguru.com` does not resolve).
   Every number is from one Mac. There is no staging, so no load, rollback or real-latency evidence exists.
5. **Readiness was declared before it was measured.** "FACULTY-READY GO" (2026-10-05), "PROD READY / 95 clips indexed"
   (Curly Tales), "n=300 calibration", "163 tests green", "sub-30 ms" were each later found false. Each false green cost a
   later session an audit to undo.
6. **Scope grew sideways while the core was unproven.** Retention streaks, daily ritual card, WhatsApp bot, canonical memory,
   second brain, healing courses, mobile apps, OKF wiki, Memgraph migration, Helm charts, 21 CI workflows. Each is partly
   built and each has its own open list.
7. **Parallel agent sessions share one working tree.** The 144-point index contamination on 2026-10-04 came from another
   session's files. Today a second session is editing the index builder in this same tree. There are 40 local branches and
   10 leftover agent worktrees.
8. **Too many documents, no single list.** `docs/` holds about 130 top-level files and `.claude/tasks/` over 100 plans, many
   named "ruthless…". Sessions spend their first hour reconciling them, and `lessons.md` is 11,841 lines.

**Stop doing (until faculty preview is live):** new retrieval techniques, knowledge-graph work, memory features, mobile store
work, new audit documents, new plan files, more than one agent session writing to the tree, and any "GO" claim without the
command that produced it.

Session "Production readiness and faculty preview" (the first-person lane; still running on 2026-10-12, ending soon) owns `backend/scripts/ops/build_first_person_index.py`, `backend/ingest/verbatim/boundaries.py`, `backend/tests/test_index_completeness.py` and `evals/gold_set_v1/`. Its work is committed (`387550fb`, `ac683b23`, `65ed0116`, `b62b535d`, `706d3197`, `66555c47`); the gold-set CSVs stay local and git-ignored.

- ✅ **v9 index (complete thoughts), pilot done, NOT recommended (2026-10-11, final numbers).** Final run is `~/mukthiguru_attribution_data/v9_dryrun_20261011/pilot_results.json` (the earlier `pilot_stdout.json` figures are superseded): `1_-cZz8YRFw` 26 → 23 clips, contained 4 → 2, voice-verified 19% → 22%; `iKkySU5r_x8` 49 → 44 clips, contained 7 → 3, voice-verified 41% → 41%. Built as `first_person_v9_pilot` (67 points, v7/v8 untouched). **Blind independent Opus judge, 142 clips (75 v8 + 67 v9, version hidden): complete at both ends 24% → 27%, good clips 13% → 15%, mean standalone value 1.16 → 1.16, risk 19% → 18%, garbled 12% → 12%.** Whole-corpus v9 would cut 1,802 → 1,339 clips for ~3 points. The stage is merged behind `--complete-thoughts` (default off). Real bottleneck: only ~1 in 7 stored clips is a clean self-contained teaching; fix = sentence-level re-segmentation with word-level speaker coverage plus ASR quality (see T-B13). Evidence: release report Addendum 6.
- ✅ **Whole-corpus clip grading finished; "do not restart `mukthiguru-backend`" hold LIFTED (2026-10-12).** The grader job completed (1,801 rows; 24 ungradable) and the backend has since been recreated several times (env experiments below); the container is healthy on its original config. Results are in T-B13. The allowlist (`config/first_person_allowlist.json`, 119 ids, `b62b535d`) is **disabled everywhere**; `.env` does not set `FIRST_PERSON_QUALITY_ALLOWLIST_PATH`.
- ✅ **Latency / rerank experiment, 29 on-topic questions x 3 repeats per arm, uncached, blind Opus judge (2026-10-12).** Harness `.work/latency/run.py` (local), results `.work/latency/arm_A3.json`, `arm_B3.json`, `judge2_out.json`. Arm A = current config; arm B = `FIRST_PERSON_LLM_RERANK_ENABLED=false` + `FIRST_PERSON_RERANK_ENABLED=true`. A: p50 7.48 s / p95 13.74 s, 16 of 87 served, Opus 8 SHOW / 6 CAVEAT / 2 DON'T. B: p50 6.92 s / p95 9.25 s, 20 of 87 served, Opus 11 SHOW / 4 CAVEAT / 5 DON'T. 0 errors in both; earlier 13-question out-of-corpus run served 0 clips in both arms. **Reading: B cuts the tail (p95 -33%) and serves more, but its DON'T share is higher (25% vs 12.5%); 14 unique clips judged, so not conclusive. Not adopted.** The ~6 s floor is the two sequential gate calls (answerability + relevance scorer), not rerank; T-C6 is the real fix.
- Note for the peer: `.env` was edited with backups (`.env.bak_pre_latency2_20261011`) and restored byte-identical after each arm; the container still runs the OLD requirements pins (litellm/openai) until an image rebuild is scheduled.
- ⏳ **Gold-set package (built, labels blank).** 505 pairs (150 on-topic questions, 40 out-of-corpus, 15 adversarial), annotator sheets 303 rows each with 101 overlap, labeling guide, `score_gold.py` (weighted kappa per dimension), `fit_threshold.py` (refuses non-human labels, prints `insufficient labels` < 299). **0 labels exist. Correction: 299 is the statistical minimum for confident items; the judge rated only 6 of 29 top clips show-worthy, so a 1% calibration needs on the order of 1,450 labelled top-1 questions (a perfect-ranking floor; see RC-E-3/RC-E-4). Treat this set as a seed and a rubric test.** CSVs are local-only (repo is public).

### 7.2 Finding in one paragraph

Clip selection is at its ceiling (production serves 7-8 of 29; ceiling at relevance 3 is 7,
LLM-labelled). The remaining gain is clip data: 8 questions fail on badly cut clips, 5 on
interview answers lost between `passages_B` and `passages_C_s1`, 1 on retrieval (overlapping
groups: 2 of the 5 sit inside the 8; do not sum). The cheapest
visible fix is presentation: citations drop `speaker_verified`, so all 685 voice-verified clips
show as "unverified clip". Calibration is at 0 adjudicated human labels and is blocked by more
than labelling. Nothing is deployed.

## 8. Handoff prompt: clip-quality / grading / serving session (written 2026-10-12, owner asked for it before ending the session)

> Paste this block as the first message of a fresh session. Everything below was checked in the session that wrote it; "unverified" is marked where it is.

**Role.** You continue the work of the Claude session that owned first-person clip quality, whole-corpus grading, the quality allowlist and the serving experiments for AskMukthiGuru. The peer session ("Agent protocols shipping and launch") owns this task list's T-A rows, CI, nightlies and Trivy. Message it before touching the same file, and commit by explicit path (`git commit -m ... -- <path>`), never `git add -A`.

### 1. Goal
AskMukthiGuru answers every question with the teacher's own recorded words (a verbatim clip plus an exact timestamp), on web and WhatsApp, with no LLM-generated teaching text at serve time. Prod-ready, faculty-ready and user-ready means: a seeker gets a relevant, complete, risk-free clip or an honest abstain; nothing is invented; "Verified" is never shown unless proven. The binding constraint is **clip quality in the data** (about 1 in 7 stored clips is clean), not selection. Owner requirement (2026-10-12): default answer is the teacher clip; the generating pipeline may run only on an explicit "personalize" request from a signed-in seeker who has a personal graph (T-B14).

### 2. Current state of the code (checked 2026-10-12)

My commits on it: `65ed0116` (allowlist feature + grader), `b62b535d` (allowlist ids), `706d3197` (task-list rows). **Not pushed to origin beyond what the owner pushed; I cannot push (permission-blocked). The owner runs `git push`.**
- Serving collection `first_person_v8` (1,801 points). `.env` first-person flags: bridge on, `LLM_FALLBACK=false`, `SERVE_WEAK_MATCH=true`, `RELEVANCE_MIN_SCORE=3`, `RELEVANCE_MODEL=anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5`, `OPENROUTER_EXTRA_ALLOWED_MODELS=anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5`. **Code defaults are the opposite** (`config.py:250,254`: fallback True, weak-match False): a deployment missing the env vars generates text. T-B14 fixes this.
- Quality allowlist feature exists (`FIRST_PERSON_QUALITY_ALLOWLIST_PATH`, fail closed, off by default). `config/first_person_allowlist.json` holds 119 ids. **Not enabled anywhere.**
- **Backend image was rebuilt and the container recreated on 2026-10-12** from the new `requirements.lock` (litellm 1.91.5, openai 2.54.0, importlib-metadata 8.9.0; pip removed from the image). Health OK, 0 restarts. The older figures (9,932 before the lock bump, 9,890 on the new lock) are superseded.

### 4. Everything tried, with the result (including failures)
| Try | Result |
|---|---|
| Whole-corpus v9 "complete thoughts" index (2-video pilot) | Blind Opus judge on 142 clips: complete both ends 24% -> 27%, good clips 13% -> 15%. **Rejected**; whole-corpus would cut 1,802 -> 1,339 clips for about 3 points. Stage merged behind `--complete-thoughts`, off. |
| Clip-level speaker recheck to bulk-mark clips verified | Centroid-only unsafe; per-window keeps only about 15%. **Not applied.** 685 of 1,801 clips verified. |
| Relevance + standalone + answerability gates (4 rounds, Opus judge) | DON'T-SHOW 42% -> 12%, mean relevance 1.67 -> 2.38, 13/13 out-of-corpus abstained. Ceiling: only 15/29 questions have any showable clip in their top 10. |
| Whole-corpus Haiku grading | 1,801 clips: A 336 / B 406 / C 1,035 / ERR 24 (1.3%). First two passes left 246 then 78 ERR (unparseable batch JSON, retried by dropping ERR rows and re-running; 24 never parsed). Haiku "complete" precision vs 290 Opus labels only 67% (recall 80%): **lenient**. |
| Serve-only-grade-A (Haiku A) | Candidate clean rate 29% -> 70% but questions with a showable clip 15 -> 10 of 29. |
| Opus second pass on the 336 A clips | **119 kept (35%)**, ids-only allowlist. Against the 290 labels: 91% clean inside (20/22, n tiny) vs 24% outside; showable questions 15 -> 7 of 29 if enforced. **Not enabled** (coverage cost). |
| Latency round 1 (42 q, uncached) | A: p50 7.56 / p95 13.9 s, 3 served. B (LLM rerank off + cross-encoder on): p50 6.25 / p95 12.46 s, 5 served. Opus 2/1/0 vs 4/0/1 (SHOW/CAVEAT/DON'T); n=8, noisy. First attempt returned "Anonymous quota exceeded" (5 per token; minting tokens is also rate-limited) -> ran with `ANON_QUOTA_ENABLED=false` and a single token. |
| Latency round 2 (29 on-topic x 3 repeats, blind Opus) | A: p50 7.48 / p95 13.74 s, 16/87 served, 8 SHOW / 6 CAVEAT / 2 DON'T. B: p50 6.92 / p95 9.25 s, 20/87 served, 11 / 4 / 5. **B has the faster tail and more served but more DON'T (25% vs 12.5%); 14 unique clips; not adopted.** The roughly 6 s floor is the two sequential gate LLM calls, not rerank. |
| Image rebuild + recreate (2026-10-12) | Healthy, pins confirmed. Post-rebuild regression check inconclusive (see section 2). |
Failures and snags worth not repeating: a first `.env` edit was denied by the permission classifier and I stopped (correct); GateGuard demands stated facts before the first Bash/file creation; the memory observer (claude-mem) has been down for about 26 days, so nothing from sessions is auto-remembered, write state to files; `pip` is gone from the image so use `python3 -c "import importlib.metadata"` for versions; `make flush-cache` fails on project `mukthiguru_clean`, use `docker exec -i mukthiguru-backend python3 - < scripts/ops/flush_cache.py`; `docker cp`d scripts in `/tmp` of the container vanish on recreate.

### 5. What I learned
1. Selection is not the bottleneck; data is. Better gates raise precision but cap coverage near 24%; only re-segmentation and better ASR move the ceiling (T-B13).
2. A cheap LLM grader is lenient on "complete thought" (67% precision); a stronger second pass plus humans is required before any allowlist is trusted. Never fit calibration on AI labels (invariant 3).
3. Cleanliness filters and coverage trade off hard: 119 clean clips = 7/29 questions. Owner must choose cleaner-but-quiet vs more answers.
4. Faster rerank trades precision for tail latency; the real latency fix is replacing the two sequential gate calls with a cross-encoder gate fitted on human labels (T-C6).
5. Measure in a single stable config: provider speed moves p50 by seconds; compare arms interleaved or with repeats, and flush the exact cache every repeat.
6. Honesty rules hold: `speaker_verified` copied from the stored clip, partial evidence is "abstained", UI never says "Verified" for related-only clips. Code defaults that differ from `.env` are a silent trap (T-B14).
7. Shared tree etiquette: explicit-path commits, tell the peer before any checkout, `.env` edits only with a backup and a restore check (`diff .env <backup>`).

### 6. Next steps, in order
1. ~~**Settle the rebuild question**~~ **DONE, no regression (see section 2).** Original text: rerun the 29-question set (3 repeats) on the new image and on `rollback-20261012-pins-litellm1.83` (needs `ANON_QUOTA_ENABLED=false` in `.env` with a backup, then restore) and compare served count and Haiku call time. If the new pins regress, roll back and tell the peer.
2. **Reply to the peer on T-B14 and take the backend half** if the owner agrees: flip code defaults (`first_person_llm_fallback_enabled=False`, `first_person_serve_weak_match=True`) with tests (`tests/test_first_person_bridge.py`, `tests/test_release_failure_injection_2026_10_05.py` reference the fallback flag), add optional `personalize_teaching={video_id,timestamp_seconds}` to `ChatRequest`, look the clip up server-side, route to the general graph only if signed in + not incognito + memory consent + non-empty graph, else a decline with a `route_decision` distinct from `first_person_abstain`, label generated text "not the teacher's words", crisis/distress first.
3. **QA-1 (hours, cheapest visible demo fix):** copy `speaker_verified`, `channel`, title into citations (`first_person_pipeline.py:1677-1704`), mapper treats a clip as verified only when the flag is true (`src/lib/firstPersonCitationMapper.ts:25`). Then QA-3, QA-5 (measure-only), QA-2; QB-1/QB-2/QB-4 need owner approvals listed in section 7.4.
4. Human spot-check of about 200 clips (review sheet without clip text in git) to put a real interval on the allowlist; optionally an Opus pass on the 406 B clips.
5. Re-measure after any serving change with the 29+13 set and a blind Opus judge, plus out-of-corpus leak check; update T-B13 and the evidence report addendum.

- The CI load test mostly measures the cache and a 2-vCPU runner; never quote its 34-38 s as production latency.

### 9.6 Deliberately not added

- **D1's Gantt timeline and owner/ETA table.** Dates are illustrative, assume teams that do not exist, mark work done that is open (C5, C7); the list gives no dates on purpose (section 0, L-GO-NEEDS-COMMAND-1).
- **D1's rubric scales (1-4 accuracy/relevance/completeness/clarity) and CSV columns.** The existing gold set already has a labelling guide, kappa scoring and overlap rows; a second schema would fork the labels.
- **D1's pyannote/Whisper "improve ASR" task and teacher balancing/rank boost.** Whisper hardening, a Whisper plus Parakeet vote and ECAPA verification already exist (M31); a teacher rank boost would bias results with no measured imbalance (K 1,051 / P 750 in v8 per invariant 23) and conflicts with source-and-voice-only identity.
- **D1's semantic cache and one-go index swap** (C9, C10; invariants 4, 8).
- **D1's SLO numbers (>= 95% coverage, > 90% precision).** Not reachable on current data (C6); RO-7 keeps the list's own targets.
- **LLM-chosen sub-span offsets and LLM-written bridges (D3).** Conflict with invariants 4, 6 and 15 (C11, C1); only template bridges or RO-3.
- **LLM edit ops as a default.** Only RB-1, behind RO-2, because of invariants 1, 2, 13.
- **Prefix-cache ordering, OTel GenAI convention pinning, price-drift tracking (D3 s5-6).** Cost is not the constraint at pilot scale (architecture doc section 8); the latency floor is the two sequential gate calls (T-C6). Revisit after T-B9.
- **CI answerability and retrieval suites on cached verdicts (D3 s6).** Premature until human labels exist (T-C1); golden-25 already proves nothing (T-C15).
- **WhisperX as a new dependency (D2, D3).** A forced aligner already exists in the repo; RB-3 reuses it.
- **D3's numeric claims (5% over-correction, 78% deletion precision, N=60 deterministic quoting, Sufficient Context +2-10 points, nondeterminism counts).** External, adjacent-task, unverified; used only to justify measuring our own rates (R-1, R-4).
- **Re-verifying D2's counts against live Qdrant** (621/2,486 on v7, 1,801 v8 points): no service access allowed; v8 figures come from the local builder report only.
- **Running any test or probe** (task rules): A7 and C16 stay UNVERIFIED-CANNOT-RUN.

Drafted by a read-only subagent that read the three reports end to end and checked D2's claims against the code on 2026-10-12; the task-list session spot-checked three of its repo claims (`config.py:187` and `:158`, `lessons.md:11883`, `attribution.py:52-53`) and found them as stated. Not checked: live Qdrant counts, the deployed Railway environment, any test result (A7 stays unverified). The suggested edits to existing rows were applied in the same commit except the last one (an owner-approved `CLAUDE.md` edit, held under RO-3). Section 9 sits before section 10 because the research session reserved that number for its speech-pipeline addendum.

## 10. Speech-pipeline lessons from WhisperLiveKit and "Multi-Stage Speaker Diarization for Noisy Classrooms" (appended 2026-10-12)

Appended by the research session on the owner's instruction; no line above this heading was
changed. (Section 9 is reserved by the task-list session for its own pending addendum.) Row ids
are prefixed `QS-`. These rows are the concrete form of T-B13 and QB-3 (re-segmentation) and
QB-1 (interview mode): they say *what* to change in the clip build and why.

**Sources.** [WhisperLiveKit](https://github.com/QuentinFuxa/WhisperLiveKit) (README,
`docs/alignement_principles.md`, `whisperlivekit/tokens_alignment.py`; read through a page
summariser, not line by line, and its VAD/buffer internals are not documented on the pages read).
[arXiv 2505.10879v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2505.10879v1), Khan et al., Stanford, May 2025
(English classroom audio; its numbers are theirs, not ours).

**Our pipeline, as read today** (`~/mukthiguru_attribution_data/mass_ingest_2026-10/stages/`,
outside the repo): Whisper large-v3 + Parakeet → word vote → optional forced alignment →
ECAPA embeddings on 2 s windows every 1 s over the whole file → agglomerative clustering with
no speaker count → cluster named against stored voiceprints → each word takes the label of the
nearest window centre → clips cut wherever the label changes.

**Measured today on `transcripts_B/` (516 videos, 297,103 words, read-only).** This is the
word-label stage. v8 was built from `passages_C_s1`, a later stage; whether that stage repairs
any of this was **not** checked.

| measurement | value |
|---|---|
| Teacher runs (unbroken same-teacher word sequences) | 8,001 |
| Runs that end at a blip of 1-3 words labelled unknown (`?`) or other (`O`) with the **same teacher on both sides** | 3,954 (49.4%): 2,632 `?`, 1,322 `O` |
| Teacher runs shorter than 2 s / 2-5 s / 5-15 s / 15 s or more | 17.6% / 24.7% / 33.1% / 24.6% |
| Words labelled unknown / other | 10.1% / 14.0% |
| Word start times that go backwards | 379, in 213 of 516 videos |
| Adjacent repeated words | 1,851 (0.62%) |

Reading: about half of all clip boundaries at this stage are label flicker inside one teacher's
continuous speech, not real speaker changes. That is a direct mechanism for the mid-sentence
clips the grading found (T-B13: 1,035 of 1,801 graded C).

### 10.1 What the two sources do that we do not

| # | Their design | Ours today | Evidence in our code |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | WLK gives each word the speaker whose span **overlaps it most**; a word crossing a boundary stays whole on the majority side | Point lookup: nearest window centre to the word midpoint, `?` if farther than 1.5 s | `run_clips.py` `label_speaker_for_words` |
| 2 | WLK keeps punctuation with the preceding text and closes lines at sentence punctuation (`--buffer_trimming sentence`) | Punctuation stage runs, but clips are cut with `display_words=None`: boundaries are label changes, a 180 s cap, a 60 s + 1.5 s-gap rule, and child clips every **150 words** | `run_clips.py` `segment_monologue`, `run()` |
| 3 | WLK makes silence its own segment and never merges it into speech; a long pause forces a boundary | Pauses matter only after 60 s of clip | same |
| 4 | WLK holds text back as "not yet attributed" until diarization covers it, then moves it whole | Unattributed words become `?` and **split the clip** | same |
| 5 | Paper: diarization error tracks speech-detection error almost exactly (Spearman 0.96); best result is a **hybrid** of a frame-level speech detector and Whisper word timestamps | No speech-detection stage before speaker embedding: windows over music, chant, applause and silence are embedded and clustered with speech | `speaker_attribution.py` `embed_windows`; CLAUDE.md invariant 7 |
| 6 | Paper: speaker confusion is 3.6% on segments over 5 s and 46.3% under 2 s | 42% of teacher runs are under 5 s; L-FP-9 admits 2-7 s interview answers as "verified by speaker centroids" | measurement above; `build_first_person_index.py` |
| 7 | Paper: automatic speaker-count estimation gave unbalanced clusters; they fixed the count. It also notes enrolment methods do better where voice samples exist | Clustering with `n_clusters=None` and a distance threshold, 30-60 min per long video (L-FP-3), although we **have** enrolled voiceprints for both teachers | `speaker_attribution.py:81-86` |
| 8 | Paper: denoising at inference suppressed quiet speakers and raised false alarms; useful only as training augmentation. Fine-tuning the embedding model on a small set made it worse | Neither is done today. Recorded so nobody adds them as a quick fix | — |
| 9 | WLK resets its cursor when a token's time goes backwards, and treats that text as volatile | 379 backward steps reach the clip cutter unhandled; Parakeet runs in 120 s chunks with 15 s overlap, a plausible source (**hypothesis, not traced**) | `run_asr.py` `run_parakeet` |
| 10 | WLK warns `auto` language detection drifts to English and that some backends give only ~1 s word timing | Whisper is pinned to `language="en"`; fine for English, wrong for any non-English video. Voted words can carry timestamps from either recogniser | `run_asr.py` |

### 10.2 Tasks

All are first-person lane work (owner of record: next session). None changes serving. Each
needs the stale-point fix first (QB-12) if it ends in an index build, and an owner-approved new
collection. Order is by expected effect per day of work.

| id | task | done when |
|---|---|---|
| QS-1 | [ ] **Absorb label blips before cutting.** A `?`/`O` run of up to N words with the same teacher on both sides and no pause is relabelled to that teacher only when a per-word overlap score supports it; otherwise it stays a boundary. N and the score rule are set by measurement on hand-checked blips, not picked. Host interjections ("mm-hmm") are the risk: sample 100 blips and listen before enabling | share of teacher-run boundaries caused by blips falls from 49.4%; host-speech rate in a 200-clip listening sample does not rise |
| QS-2 | [ ] **Majority-overlap word labelling** in place of nearest-centre lookup (source row 1) | same sample: fewer one-word label flips at turn edges |
| QS-3 | [ ] **Cut at sentence ends and pauses.** Feed the punctuated words into the cutter; a clip ends only at sentence punctuation or an explicit silence; drop the 150-word child rule (source rows 2, 3). `backend/ingest/verbatim/boundaries.py` already has sentence helpers | blind judge: "both ends complete" rises from 24-27%; median clip length moves toward the 15-60 s band where showable clips sit |
| QS-4 | [ ] **Speech mask before embedding.** Embed only windows that a speech detector and the voted-word timestamps both support (source row 5); Silero is the cheap first detector | fewer `O`/`?` windows over music and silence on 10 hand-marked videos; clustering time drops |
| QS-5 | [ ] **Score windows against the two enrolled voiceprints directly**, or fix the cluster count per video format, instead of open-ended clustering (source row 7) | agreement with a human-marked sample at least as good as today; per-video time measured |
| QS-6 | [ ] **Short answers need stronger evidence.** For clips under 5 s, verify the speaker on the whole surrounding turn, not the clip window; keep "unverified clip" where that fails (source row 6). Applies to the QB-1 interview restore | no short clip marked verified on a sub-5 s window alone |
| QS-7 | [ ] **Trace the backward timestamps** to Parakeet chunk overlap or the vote; fix at the source, and make the cutter reject a non-monotonic word list (source row 9). Check whether the 0.62% repeated words come from the same place | 0 backward steps in rebuilt transcripts |
| QS-8 | [ ] **Apply the L-FP-8 fix that is still missing**: consecutive host/unknown turns append to the pending question instead of overwriting it | interview question text complete on the Curly Tales video |
| QS-9 | [ ] Check whether `passages_C_s1` inherits the blip boundaries measured on `transcripts_B` | one number recorded here |
| QS-10 | [ ] Candidate bake-offs, only after QS-1 to QS-5 are measured: an end-to-end diarizer (Streaming Sortformer, 4 speakers; pyannote) and TitaNet-Large against ECAPA. The paper's ranking is on children's classroom audio and may not hold for ours | a decision recorded with our own numbers |

**Owner / human parts.** QS-1 and QS-6 need the T-B5 listening audit to judge host-speech risk
(reviewer still unnamed, T-A6). A new collection from any of these needs owner approval.
Non-English videos: confirm whether any are in scope before the `language="en"` pin matters.

**Not done / limits.** Nothing was changed in the pipeline. Stage scripts live outside the repo
and are not under version control. The paper's figures come from classroom recordings; they
point at where to look, they do not predict our gains. WhisperLiveKit is a live-streaming tool;
only its alignment and segmentation rules transfer to our offline build.

## 12. Clip data and serving: research rows (RC-), first-person lane, 2026-10-12

Source: six read-only research passes (one per topic A-F) with web research and our own offline measurements; full row text, sources and scripts-in-words are in the gitignored `.work/research/RC_A.md` ... `RC_F.md` (local only). Rules kept: research and rows only, no code, container, index or `.env` change; no clip text. **Every number marked THEIR is someone else's benchmark; every number marked OURS uses AI (Opus/Haiku) labels or desk estimates on small sets and ranks ideas only; none feeds calibration (invariant 3).** Source status: OPENED-PRIMARY means the primary page was read; SUMMARY-ONLY means a snippet or abstract.

### 12.0 What changes the starting order (read this first)

1. **One rebuild, not many.** RC-A-1/2/5/6, RC-C-2/3/5, RC-D-1, RC-F-1/5/6 and RC-B-1 all end in a new collection (invariant 4: new version = new collection). Batch them into ONE new collection (call it `first_person_v10`), dry-run first, snapshot, owner approval (QB-12 first: the mass-ingest driver never deletes stale points). Do not hot-patch v8.
2. **RC-D-1 first among data changes.** The shared instruction prefix "Given a spiritual teaching, retrieve relevant passages: " is added to BOTH queries and clips (`embedding_service.py:186,1000`; clips via `build_first_person_index.py:607`). The BGE-M3 card says no instruction is needed (OPENED-PRIMARY). Without it, dense AUROC (good vs not) 0.62 → 0.78 and top-1 on-topic vs out-of-corpus 0.84 → 1.00 (OURS, AI labels). **QBUG-16 ("dense cosine does not separate") is mostly this artifact; QO-3 and QB-4 (which score to calibrate) should wait for RC-D-1.** Do NOT change the shared `encode_batch` (the chat corpus was embedded with the prefix); add a first-person-only encode path.
3. **Cheap, safe, can go first (no new collection):** RC-F-2 (link and embed use different start seconds; 2-3 h), RC-E-5/6/13 (calibrator cannot certify a set with any error; 4 dp rounding bug; docstring) as code + tests only, RC-D-2 (start the answerability call at request arrival + cache it + add policy hash to the exact-cache key = QA-2 + QBUG-4; 1 day), RC-C-4 (word-level timing check; 2 h).
4. **Align-only rerun before QS-2/QS-3:** RC-C-2 + RC-C-5 (all 379 backward timestamps sit at Qwen-aligner chunk seams; 4.3% zero-length words). No new ASR.
5. **RC-B-1 before QS-1..QS-5 and any diarizer bake-off:** counting only windows fully inside the clip roughly doubles verified coverage (approx. 552 → 1,148 on `passages_C_s1`, an unverified agent-run approximation; exact v8 number needs a dry run), no new model. Needs a human listener (T-B5, unnamed).
6. **The cutter that served v8 is `speaker_diarization.build_clips_from_labelled_words` via `build_clips_v2.py`, not `run_clips.py`** (RC-A section 0). So QS-1, QS-8, QBUG-8, QBUG-9 point at the wrong script; QS-3 is mostly already done.
7. **Calibration plan was mis-sized and mis-aimed (RC-E):** the tooling fits on retrieval top-1 (`--unit top1`), not the served clip; the labelling cost is the number of SERVED clips (about 299-1,000+), not questions asked; "1,450 questions" is a perfect-ranking floor with a 753-3,740 range. A defensible faculty-preview claim from 150 human-labelled served clips is a bound of about 2-5%, not 1%. RO-5 stays OFF.
8. **Three sequential LLM calls, not two** (rerank → Haiku scorer → question-only answerability, in that order, `first_person_pipeline.py:1478,1580,1625`). An out-of-corpus question pays for all three before it is rejected. T-C6 as written (swap for an off-the-shelf cross-encoder) fails the leak guard on our data (serves 1 of 29 at 0 leaks): reframe as fast path + LLM only for the uncertain band + keep the answerability gate (RC-D-5).

At a 24-31% served share, 299 served clips need about 965-1,246 questions asked but only ~299-400 labelled. T-C1's "~1,450 labelled" conflates the two. | `fit_threshold.py` (`need` check counts all rows); T-C1, RO-1 | Plan "ask N real questions, label every served clip"; label ~50 abstentions only to estimate false-abstain rate (not in the bound) | RO-1 labelling-hours recomputed from served count; log shows >=299 served rows before labelling starts | hours / low | O: RO-1 |
| RC-E-4 | 1,450 = 299/0.207 is a perfect-ranking floor: the 6/29 has a 95% interval 8.0-39.7%, so the floor ranges 753-3,740 (OURS-COMPUTED). Power: with true risk 0.5% the zero-error 299 start certifies only 22% of the time; a 1% claim effectively needs true served risk <=0.1-0.2%. 5% (n=59-153) or 2% (n=149-386) is reachable. Exact n at delta 0.05: 1% 299/473/628/773; 2% 149/236/313/386; 5% 59/93/124/153 for 0/1/2/3 errors (OURS-COMPUTED, scipy, matches the repo bisection to 1e-15). | T-C1, RO-1; `MAX_TARGET_RISK = 0.01` (`first_person_pipeline.py:77`) | Size the batch by power; choose target risk from the power table; if 1%, write down that it needs true risk <=0.2% and ~1,000-2,000 served labels | RO-1 states target, k_max, planned n and power at an assumed true risk, pre-registered | hours / low | **O: RO-1, QO-4** |
| RC-E-5 | The calibrator certifies only a zero-error top-`n_min` prefix of the selected items: the walk starts at the first threshold with `n_selected >= n_min(0 errors)` and `break`s at the first threshold whose bound exceeds the target, so one error among the first `n_min` selected items yields no operating point and "628 for 2 errors" is unreachable (wording corrected 2026-10-13: an earlier text said it can never certify a set containing an error, which was too strong; re-read at `calibrator.py:103-117`) (QBUG-15 understates it). LTT allows any pre-declared start and multi-start at delta/|J| (§2.3.1, OPENED-PRIMARY). | `backend/evaluation/gold/calibrator.py:find_operating_threshold` (103-123), `_min_selected_for` | Add `allowed_errors k` (start at the first threshold with n_selected >= need(target, k)), or LTT multi-start; fix the warning text; k fixed before labels are seen | Test: 700 items with 2 errors in the top 300 certify at n>=628 with k=2 and fail with k=0; profile stores `allowed_errors` | 3-4 h / low | O: QO-4 chooses k |

### 12.2 Contradictions with existing rows (fix these when the rows are next edited)

- **T-B3 / invariant 23** "per-window rejects 56%": 85/150 includes the 50 controls that all pass; against the 100 unverified clips it is 85% rejected, 15% kept; the sample was `first_person_v7`, not v8. (RC-B)
- **QBUG-8, QS-1, QS-9, QS-3, QBUG-9, QS-8, T-B13:** the served cutter is `speaker_diarization.build_clips_from_labelled_words` (v2), not `run_clips.py`; the served-path blip-split figure is 36.7% (almost all `O`), not 49.4%; QS-3 is mostly already done; QBUG-9/QS-8 changes in `run_clips.py` change nothing served (v2 sets `question_context=None`); the measured residual after sentence alignment is discourse dependency, so T-B13's "more sentence-level re-segmentation" is RC-A-1/2. (RC-A)
- **QBUG-3 and the v9 `--complete-thoughts` pilot evidence:** `standalone_pass_share` 0.92 → 1.0 rests on detectors with precision 0.38-0.42 vs a 0.355 base; do not quote as quality evidence. (RC-A-3)
- **Invariant 11 (18-25 s target):** our labels put SHOW at 15-60 s, almost none over 90 s. **Invariant 7 "no Silero VAD stage":** only true for speaker embedding; `run_asr.py` uses faster-whisper `vad_filter=True`. (RC-A)
- **QS-7 / QBUG-10:** not Parakeet or the vote; all 379 are Qwen-aligner chunk seams (RC-C-2); the duplicated-words worry is not supported (0.59% repeats are already in raw Whisper). **Architecture doc line 16 ("0.7 s median offset")** is a method artifact; word-level is 0.10 s (RC-C-4). **Invariant 7 "prompt is live":** it affects only the first ~30 s window (RC-C-1), and the stage script uses a second hand-kept glossary. **QS-2** will mishandle the 4.3% zero-length words (RC-C-5).
- **QS-10 / QS-4:** the diarizer bake-off needs a licence ruling first (pyannote community-1 and Streaming Sortformer weights are CC-BY-4.0, outside the CLAUDE.md allowed list; DiariZen CC BY-NC); Silero's licence page is inconsistent and silent on music/chant. **L-FP-3 / QBUG-11:** embedding, not just clustering, dominates per-video time. (RC-B)
- **Task-list section 3, T-C6 "Now" text:** the floor is three sequential LLM calls (rerank, scorer, answerability last), not two; **T-C6** as written fails the leak guard on our data (RC-D-5); **QBUG-16** is mostly the double-prefix artifact (RC-D-1); **invariant 19** "Tier 1 = cross-encoder separation": the cross-encoder is off by default, Tier 1 is in practice the LLM rerank; **section 7.2** "selection at its ceiling" holds for top-10 (15/29) but the scorer's top-3 window reaches only 10 (RC-D-3).
- **T-C1 / RO-1:** "~1,450 labelled top-1 questions" is wrong twice (labels needed on SERVED clips only; 1,450 is a perfect-ranking floor with a 753-3,740 range; RC-E-3/4). **QBUG-15** understates it (RC-E-5) and there is a separate rounding bug (RC-E-6). **QBUG-16 / QO-3:** the core defect is the calibration unit (RC-E-1). **gold_set_v1 README step 2** (kappa >= 0.6) is too weak a gate at 1% (RC-E-9). **RO-5:** agrees OFF (RC-E-11).
- **Invariant 13 / `asr_cleaner` docstring "preserves all meaning":** partly contradicted (RC-F-1). **QBUG-12:** the link floors the padded start (up to 1.25 s early) and YouTube's keyframe snap (up to ~2 s) is a larger error than whole-second rounding (RC-F-2/3). **Invariant 6:** met by the embed, not the link. **Invariant 2 / release evidence:** the sha256 gate and `quote_fidelity` are not proof that text matches the recording (RC-F-6/8).

### 12.3 Looked for and NOT found, or not worth it at our scale

- **Not found:** any sentence-boundary or punctuation benchmark for Indian-accented English; published hallucinated-boundary rates for LLM segmenters; any Silero false-alarm rate on music/chant; any diarization or verification result on Indian-accented lectures; WER for Indian English with Sanskrit vocabulary; any Whisper word-timing drift study; context biasing in parakeet-mlx; a primary source for the YouTube watch-page `t=` format and for whether `end=` stops exactly; accuracy numbers for mlx-whisper / large-v3-turbo on Indian English; any primary source showing a few hundred pairs suffice for a calibrated cross-encoder gate; a published selective-risk study on verbatim-clip retrieval for spoken spiritual content; how NotebookLM, Ask-Sadhguru style products or podcast search cut and pad clips (no primary source).
- **Not worth it:** topic segmentation (TextTiling, C99, TreeSeg, LLM tables of contents: segments run minutes, our unit is 15-60 s); decontextualization by rewriting (breaks the verbatim rule) and proposition indexing (generated text); fastcoref as the head detector (our failure is context before the clip plus generic "you" and connectives); swapping the aligner (WhisperX/wav2vec2, MFA, MMS, CTC: Qwen aligner halves Whisper's own timing error already); Calm-Whisper fine-tuning (quarantine in RC-C-3 is cheaper); DiariZen (non-commercial weights); TS-VAD/personal VAD/target-speaker extraction (need training; enrolment scoring in RC-B-2 captures the value); NeMo MSDD/EEND/WavLM/MLX ports (no Apple-Silicon evidence); CoreML diarization ports (speed is not our constraint); denoising at inference (hurt in the classroom paper); a separate ColBERT index at ~1,801 clips; a merged single answerability+relevance call as step one (invalidates the validated 13/13 OOC classifier; RC-D-2 gets most of the latency); NLI-based answerability (not measured, no primary source it beats a relevance cross-encoder); conformal risk control, conformal alignment and prediction-powered inference for the threshold (weaker or no label savings near a 0-error tail bound); SGR binary search (517 vs 299 labels at zero errors); C2PA/PROV-O/Media Fragments/full W3C annotation JSON-LD (we host neither video nor audio; YouTube ignores media fragments); NFC normalisation before hashing until Indic clips are indexed.

### 12.4 Owner rulings these rows need

New collection `first_person_v10` batching the rebuild rows (with QB-12 first); cleaning level for `asr_cleaner` ("clean" vs "smooth" verbatim; RC-F-1); licence exceptions for CC-BY-4.0 diarizer weights and a check of Silero's LICENSE (RC-B-3/6); LLM in the cutter offline only (RC-A-7); drop-vs-serve for still-anaphoric clips (RC-A-2); paid re-validation runs (RC-D-3/4); permission to use consented preview logs for calibration (RC-E-2); QO-3 score choice after RC-D-1, QO-4 allowed errors k, RO-1 target risk and batch size (RC-E-4/5/7); preview-copy wording (RC-E-11, RC-F-7); canonical Sanskrit display spellings (RC-C-1).

### 12.5 Human time these rows need (none exists yet; reviewers are Harshodai and Venkatesh, roles not split)

Listening: RC-B-1 (first/last 2 s of every newly verified clip, about 2 h for 299), RC-C-3 (20 runs, 30 min), RC-F-4 (50 clips, about 2 h; can share the T-B5 session). Marking: RC-A-8 (30 minutes of sentence ends). Reading: RC-F-1 (50 before/after diffs). Labelling: RC-E-11 (150 served clips, two annotators plus adjudication), RC-E-9 (third person blind-relabels 60-100).

### 12.6 First-person lane self-audit (reported by that session, 2026-10-13; applied here, not re-run by the task-list session)

Corrections above come from this audit. I re-checked one claim myself (the calibrator walk, `calibrator.py:103-117`: it starts at the first threshold with `n_selected >= n_min` and breaks at the first failure; the correction holds). Everything else below is the first-person session's own statement.

- **Re-verified by that session in code or data today:** RC-D-1 prefix (`embedding_service.py:186` sets the instruction, `:1000` prepends it in `encode_batch`; query side `encode_single_full` calls `encode_batch`; clip side `build_first_person_index.py:607` uses `encode_batch`; `api/first_person.py:238` and `first_person_bridge.py:521` call `encode_single_full_async`), so both sides carry it. RC-B-1 code (`speaker_verify.py:166-171` counts any window touching the clip). RC-A-1 (`build_clips_from_labelled_words` emits a parent `_0` plus children above 200 words). RC-E arithmetic recomputed with scipy: n for 1% = 299/473/628/773, 2% = 149/236/313/386, 5% = 59/93/124/153; UCB(0,298) = 0.0100024 (the rounding bug is real); 6/29 interval 8.0%-39.7%; 299*29/6 = 1,445 (all computed, no labels needed). RC-C-2: 379 backward word-start steps in 213 of 516 `transcripts_B` files, max 16.2 s ("all at aligner seams" is the agent's claim, not re-checked). RC-B recheck: 85 of 100 unverified clips rejected, 15 kept, 50 of 50 controls pass. Labels: 103 of 290 complete, 142 at relevance 1.
- **Number provenance:** every "complete", "showable", AUROC, 0.14/0.26/0.44, 0.22/0.43 and 0.11/0.52 figure in RC-A and RC-D comes from AI (Opus or Haiku) labels, not human labels; none is calibration evidence. RC-F leak percentages (6.5%, 0.7%, 15.8%, 3.0%) are desk estimates assuming coarse ECAPA labels and correct ASR timing. RC-C-4 (0.10 s median) compares against YouTube auto-caption word tags, which is another ASR, not ground truth. Svarah WER, DER/EER figures, MedVidQA spans, segmentation F1 and label-error rates are the sources' numbers. T-B1 numbers (24% to 27%, 13% to 15%, 142 clips) are Opus labels blind to version, not human. T-B2 (1,801 vs 1,802 unexplained) is still true. T-B13 grading counts recomputed earlier (A 336, B 406, C 1,035, ERR 24); the 119-clip allowlist's 91% rests on 22 labelled clips.
- **Summary-only or unverified-by-that-session sources:** RC-A-1 MedVidQA (snippets), TreeSeg, proposition indexing; RC-C-3 hallucination paper, CrisperWhisper, Calm-Whisper; RC-D-5/6 RankGPT, Promptagator, Doc2Query-- (abstracts only), Cormack RRF (not fetchable); RC-E-9/10 Northcutt, MT-Bench, self-preference, PPI, conformal papers (abstracts); RC-F vendor "verbatim" definitions and NotebookLM behaviour; RC-B TS-VAD, FluidAudio, speakrs. RC-C-1's faster-whisper prompt-reset claim rests on a source the agent read, and the installed faster-whisper version in the pilot venv was never checked.
- **Agent provenance:** RC-A, B, C, D and F came from the five agents that failed on the rate limit and were restarted from scratch (one complete pass each). RC-E came from the first attempt, whose file was complete but whose hand-back never arrived; the session read it whole and recomputed its arithmetic. About 30 percent of file:line citations were spot-checked, not all.
- **Cold-start steps no row states:** (a) The one-rebuild recipe lives only outside the repo in `~/mukthiguru_attribution_data/v8_dryrun_20261011/v8_plan.md` (a dry run, never fully executed as written; "repo backup scripts usage unverified"). Its build command: `cd backend; M=~/mukthiguru_attribution_data/mass_ingest_2026-10; QDRANT_URL=http://localhost:6333 EMBEDDING_BACKEND=onnx_int8 HF_HOME=$PWD/.model_cache/huggingface .venv/bin/python3 scripts/ops/build_first_person_index.py --passages-dir $M/passages_C_s1 --videos-json $M/videos.json --report-dir $M/index_report_v8 --collection first_person_v8 --snap-boundaries --apply`, after copying `$M/index_report/channels.json` into the report dir; host-side venv only (invariant 18). A v10 build needs a NEW collection name and `--report-dir`, a fresh passages dir from `build_clips_v2.py` (default output `passages_C`; how `passages_C_s1` was named is unconfirmed, check its `--out-subdir`), a Qdrant snapshot of the live collection first, and a dry run (omit `--apply`) before apply; every Qdrant write needs the owner's yes (dry run, snapshot, ask, apply). (b) Rollback: set `FIRST_PERSON_COLLECTION` back to `first_person_v8` in root `.env` (back it up first) and recreate the backend (`docker compose -p mukthiguru_clean up -d --no-deps --force-recreate backend` from `backend/`); a build into a new collection never modifies v8. **No snapshot of `first_person_v8` is recorded anywhere in this list** (only the v7 snapshot name, in invariant 23): take one before any v10 work. (c) `passages_C_s1` and `transcripts_B` live in `~/mukthiguru_attribution_data/mass_ingest_2026-10/`, are local-only, and the seven RC research files and scratch scripts are in `.work/research` (git-ignored; scripts not persisted). (d) Frozen eval inputs: `.work/autoresearch_fp/{candidates.json,labels.jsonl,split.json,eval.py}` and the 29x3 harness `.work/latency/run.py` (needs `ANON_QUOTA_ENABLED=false` in `.env` with a backup, restored afterwards).
- **First-person lane confidence (their number): 6 of 10 overall.** About 8 of 10 for code locations and arithmetic; medium for the direction of findings; about 4 of 10 for the size of expected gains (coverage doubling, completeness uplift, AUROC jump), because they rest on AI labels, small n and agent runs not fully re-run, with nothing tested end to end on a rebuilt collection and no human-labelled data for any quality claim.

## 13. Platform, safety and release: research rows (appended 2026-10-12)

Written by the task-list session after four read-only research subagents (areas: serving under load and config safety; Railway, supply chain and image hardening; crisis safety, WhatsApp and observability; Supabase backups and India DPDP) read the sources and our code. **43 rows**: `RP-A1`..`RP-A12`, `RP-B1`..`RP-B9`, `RP-C1`..`RP-C12`, `RP-D1`..`RP-D10`. Each row names a file the agent read; none was run or measured. Primary sources are marked as opened or summary-only in each source table at the end of this section. Legal and clinical points are flagged for a lawyer or clinician, never decided. Nothing was found for a row without a file in our repo; those were dropped.

**What I checked myself (task-list session, read-only):** RP-A1 (the cap decorator wraps three handlers and `stream_orchestrator.py:110` creates a background task), RP-A3 (`start_railway.py:499` has `workers=2`), RP-A4 (`main.py:978` adds the faculty gate only if the code is set), RP-C1 (`wa_bot.py:1079` and `:1251` rate-limit before the backend), RP-C2 (KIRAN number in `wa_bot.py:356` and `README.md:204`), RP-C3 (`DISTRESS_DETECTIONS` is defined and read, nothing increments it), RP-D1 (`handleGoogleSignIn` has no `ageConfirmed` check; the checkbox is enforced only at `AuthPage.tsx:453` and by a CSS rule at `:963`), RP-D4 (`delete-my-account/index.ts:155` lists `memory_deletion_receipts`, which a migration calls append-only). **Not checked by me:** every web claim, the rest of each table, and every DPDP date (the primary DPDP text was not reachable by the agent; summaries only).

**Correction to the agents:** two agents said Railway config is not in the repo. `.railway/railway.ts` is tracked (see notes on RP-A10, RP-B1, RP-B7).

Memory service notes it also owns Qdrant/Neo4j/Redis and guru_* tables, purged by `/account/purge-memory` before Postgres deletion; if `BACKEND_URL` is unset the function only logs a warning and continues (`memoryWarning`), so a preview deployed without it would leave vector and graph data behind. **Source:** Our code (read) | `supabase/functions/delete-my-account/index.ts:53` (skips purge with warning), `:131-134`, `backend/app/api/memory.py:345-366` | Make the edge function fail (or return an explicit partial status) when `BACKEND_URL` is unset in production; add a test that enumerates `user_id` tables from the schema against the delete list. | Test fails when a new user-keyed table is added without being listed; deploy checklist lists `BACKEND_URL`. | 0.5 day | No. **Contradicts:** None |

Our frontend already runs its own `checkPasswordBreached` before sign-up, which partly compensates, but server-side minimum stays at 6 (project setting per CLAUDE.md audit, not re-checked). **Source:** S3 | `src/pages/AuthPage.tsx:461-466` (`checkPasswordBreached`), root `CLAUDE.md` audit section | Raise min length to 8+ in Auth settings (free, dashboard); keep the client breach check; HIBP server-side only if Pro chosen. | Dashboard setting shows min length at least 8; sign-up with a 6-char password rejected by the API (not just the UI). | 5 minutes, low risk | Owner (dashboard). **Contradicts:** Refines T-D3: min length can be raised on Free; only HIBP needs Pro. Client breach check is a partial mitigation T-D3 does not mention |
| RP-D7 | CAPTCHA: Supabase supports hCaptcha and Cloudflare Turnstile at Auth settings; docs list protection for sign-in, sign-up and password reset and are silent on OAuth; docs state no plan restriction. Our sign-up sends no `captchaToken`. Turnstile is the lighter option (no widget interaction required). **Source:** S4 | `src/pages/AuthPage.tsx:456-472` (`signUp` options without `captchaToken`) | Enable Turnstile in Supabase and pass `captchaToken` in the email sign-up and sign-in calls. | Sign-up call without a token returns an error from Supabase; with token succeeds. | 0.5 day, medium (frontend and mobile WebView testing) | Owner (creates Cloudflare account). **Contradicts:** Extends T-D3 ("no captcha") with a concrete free option. Not needed for a closed faculty preview behind an access gate; flag as post-preview |
| RP-D8 | Encrypted private backup alternatives. pg_dump `-Fc` is compressed and compatible with newer servers; pg_dump omits roles (the Supabase CLI `--role-only` or pg_dumpall covers that). A dump can be encrypted with `age` or GPG before leaving the runner, but uploading even encrypted dumps as public workflow artifacts breaks the workflow's own stated rule, so use a private bucket or a private repo instead. `backup.yml` also uses `version: latest` for the CLI, so a CLI change can break dumps silently. **Source:** S5, S1 | `.github/workflows/backup.yml:45-46` (unpinned CLI), `:20-27` gate | Pin `supabase/setup-cli` to a specific version; if a private bucket is chosen add an encrypt step and keep the key off the runner (public key only). | Workflow run with pinned CLI succeeds; decrypting the artifact on a laptop restores a scratch DB. | 0.5 day | Owner (destination). **Contradicts:** Refines N2/T-A2 only; does not contradict |

### 13.5 Validating the transformers 5 and sentence-transformers bump (before 2026-11-30)

Do it in a scratch venv and a scratch image first; never on the running container.
1. Resolve: compile the new pins (ST 6.1.0 + transformers 5.19.x, or the pair you choose) and list resolver blockers (RP-B6). Regenerate `backend/requirements.lock` the intended way.
2. Build `backend/Dockerfile.railway` (the deployed one) and run Trivy on that image; confirm the four IDs.
3. Embedding: `backend/scripts/validate_onnx_embedding.py` (ONNX INT8 vs reference vectors) and `backend/scripts/onnx_embedding_probe.py`. The runtime backend is `EMBEDDING_BACKEND=onnx_int8` (`Dockerfile.railway`), so check that tokenisation under transformers 5 gives identical token ids to 4.57.6 for a fixed multilingual (en/hi/ta/te/kn/mr) sample.
4. Retrieval: `backend/scripts/validate_onnx_retrieval.py` against a Qdrant holding `first_person_v8`; compare top-k to the pre-bump run on the frozen question set; require identical or explained diffs.
5. Reranker: `backend/scripts/validate_onnx_reranker.py` (allowlisted model `cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2`) and `validate_colbert_maxsim.py` if ColBERT is still on the path.
6. Latency: `backend/scripts/validate_onnx_latency.py`, compare to the measured p50/p95.
7. Verifier: load the pinned LettuceDetect model (`backend/services/lettuce_detect_service.py`, lettucedetect 0.2.3 allows transformers>=4.48.3) without mocks on 10 faithful and 10 unfaithful pairs; compare scores to the old stack. The unit tests mock these models, so they prove nothing here (as `.trivyignore` says).
8. End to end: run the first-person harness (`evaluation.first_person_harness`, golden-25 gate in `.github/workflows/golden25-gate.yml`) and the 13 out-of-corpus abstain probes; require 0 leaks.
9. Only then run the full backend test suite, merge, and drop the four `.trivyignore` lines (or renew each with a sourced reason).

### 13.7 Contradictions with existing rows
**Serving and config (A):**
- T-A7(3), QBUG-13, QO-10: they treat the admission cap as the capacity control; RP-A1 shows it does not cover streaming and RP-A3 shows it is doubled by 2 workers on Railway. The "8 slots held by 8 real runs" observation in T-A7 is consistent with that: it was measured on non-streaming `/api/chat`.
- QBUG-14 / T-A7(3) defer the load-harness fix until after the demo: acceptable for the red nightly, but T-B11 depends on a harness that does not hit the exact cache (RP-A6).
- QBUG-2 / T-B14 name four unsafe defaults; the faculty gate default (RP-A4) and the pool-versus-cap relation (RP-A9) are not in those rows.
- QBUG-13 says the cap has three values; the locustfile docstring (`backend/benchmarks/locustfile.py:11-12`) gives a fourth ("default 20").

**Railway, supply chain (B):**
- Invariant 4 (CLAUDE.md, "no alias swap by design"): RP-B2 argues Qdrant aliases are the only zero-downtime switch on a volume-backed host. Owner ruling needed.
- T-A7 / `.trivyignore` comment: "fixes are ST >=5.6 and transformers >=5.10". Red Hat says 5.6.0 does not fix CVE-2026-68770; search summaries say the transformers fixes landed in 5.3/5.5. Re-verify (RP-B5).
- T-B9 "health 200 `ready:true`" as proof of ongoing health: Railway's check is deploy-time only (RP-B1).
- T-A7 treats the Trivy gate as covering the deploy; the scanned Dockerfile differs from `Dockerfile.railway` (RP-B7).
- T-D5 omits data rollback (RP-B3). T-D4 omits the Railway-side hard/soft limit (RP-B8).
- Confirmed, not contradicted: T-B8's Qdrant 1.17.1 vs 1.18 snapshot problem (Qdrant docs: same or next minor only).

**Crisis, WhatsApp, observability (C):**
- T-C4 "web-verified only" vs `config/helplines.yaml:48-49`, which records a call-verified date for Tele-MANAS (2026-09-26, owner's chat statement). QO-1 already asks the question; the yaml, not the task row, is the current claim.
- QBUG-1 names only `wa_bot.py:352-357`; the same KIRAN number is also in `whatsapp_bot/README.md:204`.
- T-D2 frames the gap as a missing notifier; there is also no heartbeat rule and no crisis or abstention-reason metric (RP-C3, RP-C4, RP-C6).
- T-C9 says "Meta's AI-assistant terms"; the operative clause is section 4.7 (modified 2026-09-23) and it also restricts using platform data for AI training (RP-C7).
- T-C10 treats the under-18 gap as current; per summaries the child-consent provisions are phased to 2027-05-14. Lawyer to confirm (RP-C9).

**Data protection (D):**
- `docs/BACKUP_RESTORE.md:274` says upgrading to Pro "is explicitly rejected per project constraints"; T-D3 and the runbook treat the plan upgrade as an open owner choice. One of them is stale (RP-D3).
- T-D3 lumps min-password and leaked-password together; per Supabase docs only HIBP needs Pro, min length does not (RP-D6). T-D3 also omits the existing client-side breach check.
- T-C10 says the OAuth path is "unrecorded"; in code it is also not gated at all in sign-in mode and One Tap (RP-D1).
- N2 and T-A2 treat the destination as the only blocker; the unpinned `supabase` CLI (`backup.yml:45-46`) is a second silent-failure risk (RP-D8).
- Free-plan project pausing after 1 week of inactivity (S2) is not in any row read and bears on a preview that may sit idle.

### 14.2 Things a cold-start agent should not assume

- **V-13. Numbers have three grades and the list mixes them.** Human-labelled: none exist for
  clip relevance. AI-labelled (one judge, 29 questions from one interview): every "showable",
  AUROC, served-count and SHOW/DON'T figure. Other people's benchmarks: every DER, WER or
  accuracy number from a paper or model card. Only the first grade can justify a public claim.
- **V-14. "Fixed" means tests pass, not that a person saw it work.** QA-1 was seen once in a
  browser with the video pane black. Nothing has been checked on a deployed target.
- **V-15. No row has been verified end to end on Railway.** Every Railway fact is quoted from
  the 2026-10-11 evidence report or read from repo config.
- **V-16. Predicted, not observed:** latency after gate reordering; coverage after the rebuild;
  verified-coverage doubling from inner-window counting.
- **V-17. Not swept by any session:** the bodies of `lessons.md`, `.claude/tasks/`,
  `docs/agent/*`, most of `backend/scripts`, the mobile apps, and the frontend beyond the files
  named in rows.
- **V-18. The rebuild recipe is outside the repo** (`~/mukthiguru_attribution_data/`), was never
  run end to end as written, and no snapshot of `first_person_v8` is recorded (first-person
  session's audit). Take a snapshot and write the recipe into the repo before any rebuild.
- **V-19. The "~1,450 labels" figure is superseded** by section 12's calibration rows but still
  appears without a caveat in several places (first-person session's audit). Do not size
  labelling from it.

### 14.3 Open-source projects worth evaluating (licences read today on the project page)

Candidates, not decisions. Each needs a measured pilot on our audio before adoption. This pass
found no published results for any of them on Indian-accented English or Sanskrit terms.

| project | what it would replace or add | licence as read | caution |
|---|---|---|---|
| [wtpsplit / Segment any Text](https://github.com/segment-any-text/wtpsplit) | sentence boundaries on unpunctuated ASR text, for the cutter (RC-A rows, QS-3) | MIT | page shows no accuracy figure for ASR input; CPU use is inferred from its ONNX CPU install option; **not named in any row above** |
| [pyannote-audio](https://github.com/pyannote/pyannote-audio), `speaker-diarization-community-1` | end-to-end who-spoke-when with overlap handling (QS-10) | code MIT; pipeline gated behind user conditions and a Hugging Face token | page does not state commercial terms for the model: read its conditions first; DER figures are its own |
| [WhisperX](https://github.com/m-bain/whisperX) | wav2vec2 forced alignment as a second opinion on word timing (RC-C rows) | BSD-2-Clause | README: words outside the alignment dictionary get no timing; overlapping speech handled poorly; "diarization is far from perfect"; needs a language-specific alignment model |
| [NVIDIA Streaming Sortformer](https://huggingface.co/nvidia/diar_streaming_sortformer_4spk-v2) | diarization bake-off | CC-BY-4.0 | 4 speakers, mainly English, GPU-measured speed |
| [DiariZen](https://github.com/BUTSpeechFIT/DiariZen) | none | weights CC BY-NC 4.0 | **excluded**: non-commercial |
| [BGE-M3](https://huggingface.co/BAAI/bge-m3) (already in use) | none | MIT | card says no query instruction is needed (V-7) |

### 14.4 What this pass did not do

It did not re-run any benchmark, test suite, load test or model call; did not read the 94 RC-
and RP- rows one by one (the ten highest-risk rows were re-opened); did not verify any legal
statement; did not check helplines; did not touch code, containers, indexes or `.env`.

**Confidence, 1-10, with the reason it is not higher.**
- That the bugs and defects listed are real: **8**. The top-risk rows were re-read in code by
  two sessions; the rest rest on one reader.
- That the proposed fixes deliver the stated gains: **4-5**. Every gain figure is AI-labelled or
  borrowed; none is measured on human labels or a rebuilt collection. The first-person session
  rates its own gain estimates 4.
- That the list is complete: **6**. Named areas were never swept (V-17) and no human reviewer
  has looked at a single clip.
- That following the list reaches a faculty preview: **7**, conditional on Railway secrets and
  the reviewers' time. Public release without the human gates: not possible, by design.
- Session self-ratings for their own sections: task-list session 7, first-person session 6.

## 15. Evidence bar: what makes each readiness field "proven" (appended 2026-10-13)

Owner asked for a confidence of at least 9.7 out of 10 on every field. A score that high is
earned by evidence, not by re-reading: this section states, per field, the evidence that must
exist before anyone may report it as proven. Until every item in a field's "proven when" column
is recorded in this file with a commit id, run id, measurement or named human sign-off, the
honest report for that field is the "today" column. Scores are the research session's judgement
on 2026-10-13, not measurements. Ids are `E-`. Rows named are the working rows; this table adds
no new work, it sets the bar for calling work finished.

**Release gates, in order (referenced by the cold-start prompt).** Gate 1 locally proven: E-1
and E-2. Gate 2 Railway started, only on the owner's explicit go since services cost money from
the minute they start: E-3. Gate 3 faculty ready: E-4 (this is "Faculty GO", section 4).
Gate 4 user ready: E-7, with E-5 measured. E-6 and E-8 are a later phase and do not block
Gates 1-3. Every report says which gate we are at and what is missing for the next.
